It can also explain fluctuations inside a circuit without uniquely locating the source of a measured behavior. Comparing the three papers requires keeping each claim with the mathematical or experimental test that supports it.]{.key-statement}

A short calculation then shows how a common position error can make cells with different tuning change together. Comparing that calculation with each paper's evidence and related studies identifies what a joint experiment would still need to measure.

### The three papers constrain maps, dynamics, and movement

Location: line 7, anchor `sec:SYNTHESIS-QUESTIONS`

First content kind: `prose`

Opening passage line: 9
Closing passage line: 25

Paragraphs in source order (complete `PARAGRAPH_REVIEW.json`):

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p9 sha256 0b80f24a27e581a9c2f5e86fe66ccc8e2ca7aaf40b5983a4a102100b07388aff -->
p9, lines 9-9; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p5; next: p11

> For a circuit representing position, three questions arise:

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p11 sha256 a9a4d7fa3d020076c9f6f82d4de4ee3f54b1dd07ac0833e9a89b6b2bc55a7da3 -->
p11, lines 11-11; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p9; next: p12

> **Spatial map:** What activity pattern corresponds to each position? The place-field model addresses this question.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p12 sha256 1ab2eef073fbfc193d9f2724f85d531095153a9491777e56d38a4f564305f00f -->
p12, lines 12-12; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p11; next: p13

> **Memory motion:** How does the represented position evolve when there is no corrective input? The persistent-activity model addresses this question.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p13 sha256 3c8bc1157fe708f7af2b8d416c696bb1cb5b548a00af076d59a022f20713d336 -->
p13, lines 13-13; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p12; next: p15

> **Observed movement:** How does a downstream physical system transform that changing signal into an observable movement? The eye-drift model combines a candidate account of memory motion with a detailed account of this transformation.[^s-three]

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p15 sha256 5f0d743f2d1f49e3a96956d00be8a1bf6f9a70134b740a7f87c145add2891d5f -->
p15, lines 15-15; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p13; next: p17

> The objects differ even when the word position appears in all three. In 2012, $\theta(t)$ locates a network state on its attractor. In 2022, the internal eye-position command and actual eye angle are separate quantities linked through motor neurons and mechanics. In 2025, $x$ locates an animal in an environment, and $h_i(x)$ is a spatial input function assigned to a cell. The Gaussian process does not update a remembered $x$ through time.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p17 sha256 3bce5cfb23a3680a57742cebd05e7ee506a21490d771b6e569e19be03db625af -->
p17, lines 17-17; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p15; next: p19

> The [guide map](ch-persistence.html#fig-foundations-paper-map) gives a visual comparison of these three questions.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p19 sha256 9960784fd6b96a12fbf5b8855619bf1a26dae079bce219c3b96e0c64ec075ad2 -->
p19, lines 19-19; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p17; next: p23

> <div class="table-responsive">

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p23 sha256 aac32651b10f567c461b9b4f255d6fb1fa6859b5368d8bd9a51af920ab21cf23 -->
p23, lines 23-23; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p19; next: p25

> </div>

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p25 sha256 37ffdd708ba8474c6b1445eee3bb7c1ed842b4c97da9f56fa84d04086d56644c -->
p25, lines 25-25; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p23; next: p29

> The 2012 and 2022 connection is direct: neural noise can move a maintained eye-position command, which then drives muscles. The 2022 result provides evidence for a substantial shared source before the final motor neurons and shows that integrator diffusion is sufficient within the constructed model. It does not measure the circuit's Fisher information or test equality in the 2012 bound. The place-field model describes spatially irregular tuning curves. To connect those curves to memory dynamics, we must also specify how the represented position moves.


Claim-bearing content: table

> Suppose a network maintains a one-dimensional estimate $x(t)$ and cell $i$ has a fixed expected-rate function $f_i(x)$. Its time-dependent expected rate is

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p35 sha256 13140d91d0e5f7bc229ee55ffed6ea355a7c2b9331c486aad0230fb5e699327e -->
p35, lines 35-35; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p29; next: p37

> [Here $f_i$ describes spatial tuning and $x(t)$ describes dynamics. A source of time variation has entered even though $f_i$ is fixed. Actual spike counts add another layer of variability around the expected rate.]{.key-statement} This construction is a guide calculation connecting the papers' objects, not a model tested jointly by the three studies.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p37 sha256 26ec0a2ba2dd9d70b8a18d86e0a17bc2c5aa95313651452ae8db960664f8c046 -->

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p44 sha256 84605c14ed46c29ad7def1fcb1f9ae4615ec24f004367b7e54f6a6a7710a93e4 -->
p44, lines 44-44; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p37; next: p52

> To leading order, the change is $\delta R_i=f_i'(x_0)\delta x$. Multiplying the centered changes for neurons $i$ and $j$ and averaging yields

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p52 sha256 999290bb61c28849dadda74211bbbac06315dce9f77c1e44aab3636d71d726ea -->
p52, lines 52-52; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p44; next: p56

> This covariance concerns expected-rate changes caused by the common represented displacement, conditional on the starting position. It is not a formula for every covariance in a spiking recurrent network. It neglects higher-order changes in tuning and additional private or shared fluctuations. Near a threshold or over a large spatial displacement, the linear approximation must be revisited.

<!-- pedagogy-unit: content/chapters/06-synthesis.md::sec:SYNTHESIS-EVIDENCE -->

### Each model needs a test matched to its claim

Location: line 89, anchor `sec:SYNTHESIS-EVIDENCE`

First content kind: `prose`

Opening passage line: 91
Closing passage line: 91

Paragraphs in source order (complete `PARAGRAPH_REVIEW.json`):

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p91 sha256 bf26aa5ad95f5aa3bc6f807fedf818b62a3e2c4b2b3ae9dfd59c6b4ec7d43255 -->
p91, lines 91-91; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p87; next: p93

> The shared-position calculation predicts how rates would change together. Establishing that connection requires measurements of those changes as well as the spatial maps. Each paper tests a different part of the proposed connection:

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p93 sha256 08c5b1584f195f6b0470fd74fe62b5e7a6b74a88c5e5b461e8df1efc516588ad -->
p93, lines 93-93; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p91; next: p95

> **Place-field statistics:** A model that matches one distribution may have enough adjustable parameters to do so for several different reasons. The place-field paper gains support by fitting arrangement parameters and then comparing additional features, including slices and shape statistics. The separate firing-rate multiplier used in some comparisons should remain visible when counting how many quantities were predicted without further fitting. A successful high-threshold formula is also a statement about its limiting assumptions; finite-threshold simulations and the original recordings provide different kinds of checks.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p95 sha256 74808e1ca267c6539fddf1e8c68152214e385a4e8faeb35d526f50f2c20490e9 -->
p95, lines 95-95; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p93; next: p97

> **Eye drift:** The eye paper calibrates a movement-to-firing relationship, inverts it, and compares the resulting single-neuron displacement predictions with measured displacement. Shared covariance then carries information that independent private fluctuations cannot supply under the decomposition's assumptions. The additional MSD comparison tests the time structure: increasing stationary motor-noise amplitude cannot by itself reproduce the accumulating upstream signal. Calibration validity, source correlations, two animals, and horizontal movement remain part of the result's scope.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p97 sha256 bea6060b31430bed48ff70aa2cf55916410d6839d4bc94fd293b81aa8efab917 -->
p97, lines 97-97; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p95; next: p101

> **Memory stability:** The 2012 inequality is a mathematical constraint rather than an experimental fit. Its derivation specifies what noise and dynamics it covers. An experimental comparison would need estimates of diffusion, the relevant time constants, and the appropriate instantaneous information rate, all under compatible conditions. Agreement with $1/N$ scaling alone would not test the entire inequality, since several mechanisms can share that scaling.


Claim-bearing content: none

<!-- pedagogy-unit: content/chapters/06-synthesis.md::sec:SYNTHESIS-RELATED -->

### Related studies change assumptions and leave circuit choices open

Location: line 99, anchor `sec:SYNTHESIS-RELATED`

First content kind: `prose`

Opening passage line: 101
Closing passage line: 101

Paragraphs in source order (complete `PARAGRAPH_REVIEW.json`):

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p101 sha256 2fa4f402fb3c9140a5e2737da457f19171d7f49686a14d7e5e2060ddbf538125 -->
p101, lines 101-101; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p97; next: p103

> Related studies test the assumptions and ask questions the three papers leave open:

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p103 sha256 9fb495d78d188dee3a9a3498ca426138e21b7a64f2850e2231757468224f9ccb -->
p103, lines 103-103; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p101; next: p105

> **Integrating movement:** The eye-position account begins with Seung's line-attractor model. Aksay and colleagues then used recordings and perturbations in goldfish to constrain integrator circuitry. These establish the dynamical problem and experimental architecture behind the 2022 model. Neither supplies the macaque displacement-variance estimate. Burak and Fiete's earlier grid-cell study examines [[**path integration**]{.definition-term custom-style="Definition Term"}, updating a position estimate from movement.]{.definition-explanation} In its model, velocity drives recurrent population activity, and errors accumulate in the position estimate. Its accuracy ranges belong to the simulated networks and parameter choices, rather than all biological grid circuits.[^s-attractors]

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p105 sha256 246e640967b354dad7572f93f73a8a92bf512c987cd27a6e3debb2ef10642eab -->
p105, lines 105-105; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p103; next: p107

> **Storage capacity and changing synapses:** Noise-driven storage also connects to memory capacity. Koyluoglu and colleagues ask how noise limits recall as the delay and number of stored items increase. Their comparison with human data shows why direct storage of several independent values need not capture observed behavior; transformations before and after storage alter the performance prediction. Seeholzer and colleagues examine how short-term changes in synaptic strength affect diffusion and systematic drift. These papers change the storage assumptions rather than invalidate the 2012 calculation inside its original model.[^s-extensions]

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p107 sha256 35ecf69af1e00f263011e694a4578723a886e6b09ba2380aba18ba9c4469a0de -->
p107, lines 107-107; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p105; next: p109

> **Recordings from larger environments:** For place fields, Eliav and colleagues' long-tunnel recordings and Harland and colleagues' large-arena recordings supply the multiple-field, multiple-scale observations. Yartsev and Ulanovsky supply the three-dimensional bat recordings. The 2025 paper reanalyzes those existing data, so its agreement across dimensions is not three newly collected experiments under one protocol. The later study by Maimon and colleagues directly compares CA3 and CA1 in large tunnels: predominantly single fields in CA3 contrast with multiple fields in CA1, while field sizes are similar. That distinction supports treating the CA1 statistics and the upstream spatial circuit as related but separately constrained parts of the explanation.[^s-place-data]

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p109 sha256 fff18c25b24d0342b3eabc37eddf65c6c3c8fc43ce63b188e7df7e3d95a75cc7 -->
p109, lines 109-109; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p107; next: p111

> **Connectivity and learning:** Gaussian spatial statistics do not identify a unique pattern of connections. Druckmann and colleagues report structured synaptic connectivity between hippocampal regions. Bittner and colleagues show how electrical events in [[**dendrite**]{.definition-term custom-style="Definition Term"}s, the branching input structures of neurons,]{.definition-explanation} can induce changes in synaptic strength and form CA1 place fields. Structure and learning can therefore be present even when a random-function model describes many aggregate field statistics. The model's success leaves open which circuit mechanism produces these maps. Input, connectivity, learning, or perturbation measurements could distinguish the possibilities.[^s-wiring]

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p111 sha256 0ff5e5f6c91d8070a3687305852c8017a4c42373f6fa5489195fa9f6d61ba483 -->
p111, lines 111-111; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p109; next: p115

> **Benefits for perception:** The source and use of eye motion are different questions. Nghiem and colleagues combine human eye tracking with a model of how eye movement affects visual signals to explain why an intermediate amount of fixational motion can help fine visual discrimination. A movement that partly originates from internal noise can still have useful sensory consequences, and feedback can regulate its statistics. Benefit for perception does not uniquely identify the neural generator.[^s-acuity]


Claim-bearing content: none

### A joint test would compare tuning maps with population fluctuations

Location: line 113, anchor `sec:SYNTHESIS-NEXT`

First content kind: `prose`

Opening passage line: 115
Closing passage line: 122

Paragraphs in source order (complete `PARAGRAPH_REVIEW.json`):

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p115 sha256 b24788061e16be10b5024058afee1e1e0efb572d33bf5c409f8b22b85e6775da -->
p115, lines 115-115; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p111; next: p117

> [A joint test needs tuning maps and fluctuations from the same population.]{.key-statement} A possible sequence is:

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p117 sha256 dcf643870dff7a8c4d647743e1e653d482f30cc97b7b4b18c08365b0c2bfe28e -->
p117, lines 117-117; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p115; next: p118

> Estimate the tuning maps and their uncertainty.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p118 sha256 8abed503bea87e5f7ca0fa95cefe7acf4ad69e22af38581e2701b568c035b4ca -->
p118, lines 118-118; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p117; next: p119

> Measure fluctuations when external signals provide little correction of the represented position. Test whether their covariance aligns with the changes implied by the tuning maps.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p119 sha256 ba5c6c1493d5d94d8f7d9010d5ef5bdd1e6bb4a4c6f7a38da8f695dcf749f75f -->
p119, lines 119-119; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p118; next: p120

> Fit diffusion only over a range where the local approximation and measurement corrections are justified.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p120 sha256 7fb58465a9c8a72b3907619e2e542823ad8d388d2c66ccdd3badfce559784de2 -->
p120, lines 120-120; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p119; next: p122

> **If comparing behavior,** include the physical or neural filters between the representation and the measurement.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p122 sha256 a489b7cf52f5d0822bcc1ce8a1afccded7049f1d530356abc4543e6298f5bd46 -->
p122, lines 122-122; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p120; next: none

> This would ask whether one system satisfies both spatial-statistics and dynamical-stability constraints. [A static Gaussian-process fit alone cannot supply the diffusion coefficient. A diffusion fit alone cannot establish the distribution of fields.]{.key-statement} A joint success would be more restrictive, while a mismatch would point to a particular assumption to change: spatial input statistics, recurrent maintenance, the observation filter, or their coupling. This is proposed follow-up work, not a replication claimed by this guide.
